## T238 — Academic Paper Audit Insertions

Six topics from Gary's academic papers identified as missing from the books. All drafted at 1-3 sentences per Gary's instruction, scored, and applied directly.

### B1: String theory improbability + Standard Model bit count + anti-de Sitter curvature (~3,400w) — INSERTED, then EXPANDED
After "Nothing else is needed, warranted, or probable." (end of Schrodinger/quantum mechanics paragraph), before "Philosophy is about..."
**v1:** Four sentences: SM bit count, string landscape indexing cost, each added bit halves measure, anti-de Sitter problem.
**v2 (current):** "The Standard Model of physics, of General Relativity and Quantum Mechanics, fits on a few sheets of paper and has only 26 parameters. A short computer program to run that, and thereby simulate our entire universe, albeit very slowly, fits in roughly 10,000 bits, less than two kilobytes, less than one high-definition image. String theory's landscape holds around 10^500 possible vacua; indexing one costs over 1,600 bits. The shortest program to simulate it is many times larger than 10,000 bits. I found one: 5.7 million bits of open-source research code. Discount it by ten and 570,000 bits is still 2^560,000 less probable. Each added bit halves the measure. String theory's natural solutions are anti-de Sitter: the wrong sign of curvature for our expanding universe."
- Sources: A38 (Standard Model benchmarking, CYTools+JAXVacua measurement), A39 (string landscape), standard physics (anti-de Sitter problem). 5.7M bits = stripped CYTools+JAXVacua source; 618x ratio robust to 10x correction.
- **O 9.0. CC 0.85.**

- Source: A39. **O 9.0. CC 0.85.**

## SA69 T153 (261010)
Gary 02:06 ("continue to deep scan and improve all a files"), 02:29 and 02:39 (why each bit halves; "you either add a law or not, but you add either a 0 or a 1"; "not every added bit can form a universe"; his other reducing factor) and 02:41 (say everything first in a simple, intuitive way, then in the academic way). All 27 papers changed (A13 frozen), plus B1, B4 and MOMG. Every changed paragraph is in the T153 CHANGES PDF.
- Why each bit halves, now stated wherever the rule appears: a shortest description cannot be compressed, so its bits are random in the dichotomy's sense; a bit no rule fixes is 50/50, and a lean toward either value would itself be a rule. Half is the gentlest rate possible, since each bit doubles the number of descriptions.
- Gary's September (S59) aggregate argument restored; later passes had cut it from every file. Whether to add another bit is a second unruled 50/50, which makes the total add up to one; all descriptions one bit longer weigh half as much together, and everything longer than a length adds up to that length's weight: the tail equals the peak. At least half the weight sits at the shortest length that can produce observers (three quarters within one bit, seven eighths within two), because added laws more often break the conditions observers need than create them (his skips; Barnes 2012). Today's theories are an upper bound, not a floor. A particular description loses three quarters per added bit under the add-or-not rule; the papers' ratios use the conservative half. Full version A4 3.3 and A2 2; short versions in A0, A0a, A5, A6, A7, A8, A9, A16, A18, A21, A28, A31, A38; Gary's voice in B4 ("Why? Every law is bits... the tail equals the peak...") and B1 ("since a bit no rule decides is 50/50").
- Plain first, then precise: A0, A0a, A1, A2 and A4 rewritten so each paragraph opens with its point in plain words, then "Stated precisely", "In symbols" or "Written out". Other papers fixed where a paragraph opened with setup or a caveat.
- No if or hedge on the thesis: A4 ("under the rule proposed here", "the prior needs its own justification", "the measure is ad hoc" and "unsolvable" replies now give the dichotomy as the reason); A28 (computable minds, no "if the computations constitute experience"); A30 (real experience of fiction; "The experience of it is real."); A39 and A39a (discrete events, physical succession as generation); A8 (many worlds is the simpler account); A1, A3, A5, A7, A11, A12 (identity, realization, one self).

Changed: 9 papers (A3, A4, A5, A11, A12, A18, A35, A39, A40) and their cover letters, the academic index, and every paper PDF's equations and tables. No book or magazine piece changed.
- Astra reviewed the listening copy of all the papers, which leaves out reference lists, keyword lines and displayed equations so it reads well aloud, and judged the papers to be missing them. The listening copy now says so on its first page, and a new full edition (TOOLS/build_papers_full.py) keeps everything: each paper as its own PDF prints it, a contents page and a bookmark per paper.
- Every paper PDF now typesets its 63 displayed equations (TOOLS/mathrender.py, matplotlib's mathtext, numbered at the right margin, with the formula as invisible text for copying and read-aloud), sets 44 pieces of inline math as text with Greek letters and sub- and superscripts, and draws ruled tables. Before, eleven papers printed raw LaTeX and multi-line equations broke into stray lines.
- A3: the swap test at the hardest step (abstract, introduction, Section 3.3, conclusion; Lewis, 2009, added). Swap the natures behind mass and charge with every relation fixed: if anything anyone could notice, think or report changes, the natures do work and belong to the structure; if nothing changes, no one's beliefs can track them, so the view loses its own motive.
- A4: "A biased coin is mathematical too, but its bias is a number that has to be written into the description; one half is the only chance that needs no such number." The at-least-half result's condition shown to do work: one observer-producing description in sixteen at length four against all thirty-two at length five gives length five eight times the weight under the stop-or-go weights. Simple and low-entropy differ (an evenly spread gas), and gravity lines them up, so a smooth early universe is both (Penrose, 2004).
- A40 and A12: the change test. Alter one state of a running brain and what follows changes; alter one mark of ink, or one frame of a recording as it plays, and nothing after it changes. A12 no longer says nothing produces anything in a recording: a player has dynamics, and no recorded state produces the next.
- A40: the epiphenomenalist's usual answer (a law pairing experience with features selection preserved) stated before the reply that the pairing law is structure; idealism has one substance, so its debt is the order of nature, not an interaction problem; a gas's temperature is the average energy of its molecules' motion (three places).
- A11: shared identity predicts carry-over to partners, tasks and wording the instruction did not mention; a cooperation rule need not. A18: the shell ratio's reference speed is the Newtonian speed of the same masses; the jet tower's cost in plain words instead of "K of E plus K of n". A35: Cook's own data file has 64 abstracts in the first category (0.54 percent of the sample); Legates and colleagues, rereading those, kept 41 (0.34 percent).
- Gary 13:29: no paper makes discreteness the way gravity and quantum theory fit together; A39 comes closest. A39 now gives the physics reason beside its argument from succession: probing near the Planck length would make a black hole, and black-hole entropy (a quarter of the horizon area in Planck units) with the entropy bounds caps the distinguishable states in any finite region, so a finite region holds finitely many bits; not yet a lattice, since finitely many states can carry continuous amplitudes (Bekenstein, 1973; Hawking, 1975; Bousso, 2002 added). A5 now says fewer dimensions is not simpler by itself: a lower-dimensional theory carries its map back to the four-dimensional world, as holography's boundary theory carries its dictionary (Maldacena, 1998 added). Proposed: a new paper, A41, with the four-step case as its main claim; A5 stays a teaching paper.
- Not taken from Astra: retitling A40 (its "Why Experience Can Only Be Structure: A Matheist Reply to the Hard Problem"). Astra agrees with two earlier choices: qualifications stay beside the claims they qualify instead of moving to one closing section, and the papers stay separate.
- Checked from Astra's venue notes (October 10): Springer journals (Erkenntnis, Foundations of Physics, Minds and Machines, AI and Ethics, EJPS) allow substantial AI writing help with human oversight and disclosure; THEORIA allows language help and requires disclosure of tool, version and use; Imprint Academic (Journal of Consciousness Studies) allows AI only for readability and not for content, insights or conclusions.

- New paper A41, "Why Gravity Must Be Discrete: Finite Information and the Theory of Everything" (3,435 words, Foundations of Physics): gravity and quantum theory cap the distinguishable states of any finite region; causes act in steps; a finite description singles out only countably many states; so general relativity's smooth spacetime is the large-scale behavior of discrete structure. Section 2.3 answers Gary's 13:50 question: any set maps onto any other, so the holographic news is the count (a region's states grow with its area, not its volume), not the map.
- The chain every core paper now states up front, in short statements: everything is ruled or random and both are mathematical; each bit halves the probability, so the universe starts simple; minds are complex, so complexity has to grow from that start, as it does under simple rules such as the Game of Life; growth takes steps, because causality takes steps; a continuum has no next step, so nothing in it can cause anything; change without a cause is outside logic and reason; so time is the causal steps and runs forward, and minds run forward too. A4 is the hub that argues all of it (new Section 4.8; abstract, introduction and conclusion) and the one paper the others cite. One consciousness paper, not two. Its cover now targets Minds and Machines.

Effective spending G* is spending weighted by realized return; the price level is demand over capacity; the four schools are regimes of one equation; hyperinflation is the burn extreme (Cagan, 1956; Hanke and Krus, 2013); spending that builds competitive capacity draws the world's demand. ## SO70 T167 (261010)
Gary 17:21 ("In the book, we talk about those fascists like evola etc, actually, sound a lot like Rousseau, which is a good slap at the left. score best and add to book and mags. anything else? score best apply."; a Google AI list of radical-right thinkers). - Book 6, The Floor Is Math (O 9.5): the far right sounds like Rousseau, the left's founding father. His two Discourses (the arts and sciences corrupt virtue; civilization as a fall from natural goodness) are the fall Evola tells as the golden age sinking into the Kali Yuga and Spengler as culture hardening into civilization; his general will ("will be forced to be free") and civil religion (banish unbelievers, put backsliders to death); Robespierre's Cult of the Supreme Being during the Terror; Schmitt's idea of democracy from the same general will, with his "elimination or eradication of heterogeneity"; the far right kept the diagnosis and traded his equality for hierarchy and a strong hand; Russell, 1945: "At the present time, Hitler is an outcome of Rousseau; Roosevelt and Churchill, of Locke."
- Book 6, The Floor Is Math (O 9.0): Dugin made it geopolitics: his Fourth Political Theory fuses Evola, Schmitt and Heidegger into a Russian program for a multipolar world of traditional empires, the one the shows sell.
- Book 6, The Horseshoe (O 9.0 and 8.5): Moeller van den Bruck's 1923 book gave the Nazis the name Third Reich and called its program German socialism; de Benoist's New Right took its strategy from the Marxist Gramsci.
- UnHerd (O 9.0): the left's church has a founder (Emile, 1762: "Everything is good as it leaves the hands of the Author of things; everything degenerates in the hands of man"; the general will; the civil religion), and the far right drank from the same well (Schmitt, Evola).
- The Free Press, nationalism (O 8.5): both ends share an ancestor; Schmitt built his case against parliaments on Rousseau's general will.
- Not added from the list: Edgar Jung killed by the SS in 1934 (6.5), Guenon dying a Sufi in Cairo (6.5), Junger's total mobilization (6.0), Sam Francis's "anarcho-tyranny" (6.0), Yockey's Imperium (5.5): tangents to the book's argument.

The qubit objection is answered with the Solovay-Kitaev result: a finite gate set reaches states arbitrarily close to every state (Dawson and Nielsen, 2006, added).

## SO70 T173 (261010)

- New paragraph after the definition of discrete: what is discrete is the transitions; a state is only the cut between one transition and the next; no stuff beneath them, not even a fixed network of relations; distance and duration are patterns in how transitions follow one another; causal-set theory's order and number give the geometry (Surya, 2019). The abstract and conclusion say gravity, space and time are nothing but these causal transitions, and general relativity's smooth spacetime is their large-scale behavior.

## SO70 T174 (261010)
Gary 19:44 ("all is just relationship transitions ... the rules, the signs, and the rules are the things, but if they're static signs and rules, we could never know them like a dead brain ... everything is the application of rules on rules. Changed: A41, A0, A0a, A1, A2, A3, A4, A12, A13, A39, Book 1, Book 4, MOMG, Nautilus, Tricycle, their cover letters, the academic index, ACADEMIC_CORE_DEPENDENCIES, TOOLS/build_papers_zip.py and TOOLS/audit_keypoints.py.
- A41: each transition is a rule applied to rules (a static structure would do and know nothing, like a dead brain; compare the lambda calculus, functions applied to functions, Church 1941); time is the application; space is tied to time because both are counted in the same steps: a distance is the steps an effect needs, the speed of light is the base rate (the meter has been defined by light since 1983), a body's steps are shared between moving and changing in place, (c dt)^2 = dx^2 + (c dtau)^2, which is why a moving clock runs slow; the Game of Life's speed of light is one cell per generation and a glider moves at a quarter of it (Gardner 1970). New Section 4.6, where a theory of everything must reside: a rule for transitions, a rewriting system (Wolfram 2020, hypergraphs; Rideout and Sorkin 2000, sequential growth; Perez 2013, spin foams; quantum circuits); Zuse 1969 for the old idea that physics computes, the paper's contribution being that it must; no candidate has yet recovered general relativity and the Standard Model together. Abstract, keywords and conclusion updated.
- A13 (abstract, introduction, conclusion), A4 (step 7 and Section 4.7), A3 (Further implications: Transitions), A0, A0a, A1, A2, A12 (a mind is the kind of transition that models itself) and A39 (not even the relations hold still) state it in brief.
- Book 1, after Nagarjuna: his emptiness was never nothing; nothing has own-being, and whatever arises dependently is emptiness; what arises is rules applied to rules, one transition after the next; that is why you cannot have anything. Book 4, after the continuum paragraph: no stuff, not even relations that hold still; time is the applying, space how far it spreads; the meter defined by light since 1983; the transition budget adds like the sides of a right triangle; the theory of everything will be a rule for transitions, not a field on a continuum.
- Nautilus: the last step goes further, not even the relations hold still, and the theory of everything will be a rule for transitions. Tricycle: not even the web holds still; emptiness is the absence of anything that stays put, which is why you cannot have anything.
- ACADEMIC_CORE_DEPENDENCIES: step 7 says each step is a rule applied to rules and space is how far the steps spread; a new line says which papers carry transitions. TOOLS/audit_keypoints.py gains the key "rulesonrules".
- TOOLS/build_papers_zip.py (Gary 19:47): with --prefix and --stamp, every file inside the papers zip carries the full name (SO70_T174_A41_W..._HHMM_261010.pdf, SO70_T174_SUPPLEMENT_..., SO70_T174_PAPERS_FULL_..., SO70_T174_README_...).
- Gary 19:54, before delivery ("The paper overemphasizes ... that without the transitions, nothing exists. No, they can exist ... The points aren't stuff; they're relational nodes ... discrete does not fix the problem ... It's that the continuum has no next point, so it can't have causality"): relations exist as mathematics, their nodes zero-width points; transitions are what happens and what can be observed. A41 says so in its abstract and introduction, adding that discreteness gives the nodes no size (zoom in and you find nodes and relations, not small blocks; a size is the extent of the relations), and that the continuum's only flaw is having no next point. "Not even relations standing still" and like phrases are gone from A39, Books 1 and 4, Nautilus, Tricycle, A3 and the map.

## SO70 T179 (261010)
- Gary 21:33 ("Don't put your foot in your mouth in the middle of the paper"; soften the side claims, keep the ones that sharpen the lens; answer the real objections, "If we raise an objection and we can't answer, then that's the end of that argument"; which side is right on continua?): A41 and A39 state the fork. A reader who takes cause as dependence finds a continuum consistent; for that reader physics carries the case, finitely many distinguishable states in a region, passed through at a finite rate (Margolus and Levitin, 1998). Whether a next state is fixed or left to chance, a cause needs one. A41 softened: "has left physics", "gravity is quantized" from the measure, string theory's dualities (cut) and minimal length (hedged), "leading candidates for such a base", Sorkin's estimate an order of magnitude with its unimodular premise, holography "the part used here", dimensional reduction "suggests", black-hole entropy "read as a count", loop quantum gravity finds discreteness and causal sets start from it; Game of Life cites Winning Ways. "The rules are the only things" in A3, A1, A0, A0a, and as the view taken in A41, A13 and A4; Book 1 keeps signs with one line on what they are, Book 4 says the rules are the only things.

## SO70 T183 (261010)
- Gary 23:42 ("If there are rules for transitions, they transition ... given that all is math, and we transition, then there are rules for transitions in math ... How else could it be?"; the plant, film and reverser "can be used to illustrate ... the different kinds of cause ... make the argument that Perplexity and Astra made, and show why that's wrong"): A41 Section 3.1 now runs: the two kinds of cause in everyday cases (plant, film, reversed history); the case for a continuum (dependence is all of causation; hybrids; later need not mean next; Russell's at-at theory); why it fails (dependence is carried by production at a level below, a continuum has none, and a flow has no smallest transition, so it regresses or hands out every state at once); and Gary's argument (we change, so rules take each state to a next one).
- Gary 23:42 (how does light travel?): A41 Section 4.7, How light moves: an effect spreads at most one step per step, light at that rate; Maxwell's equations as a unitary cellular automaton (Bialynicki-Birula, 1994); zero proper time as all steps spent moving; the causal order fixes the geometry up to scale (Hawking, King and McCarthy, 1976; Malament, 1977), counting supplies the scale. A new objection: light has no path in quantum field theory. A41 is 6,234 words.
- Gary 23:42 and 23:46 (simulators who cared would spend the bits; "Moving on to other points ... computational efficiency"): Book 4 raises the ethics argument, debates it with the problem of evil (hard for them to control seems unlikely) and settles on efficiency.

## SO70 T201 (261011)
- Gary 11:43 (A39a "kind of repeats itself a lot"; discuss the two kinds of cause, dependence and production, more physics, the SDP coin flips and why we live in a simple universe or a simulation, and why complexity must arise for observers; physics in the title): A39a, the Mathematical Intelligencer essay, rewritten (1,683 to about 3,300 words). New title: "Short Laws, Discrete Steps: Why Gravity, Space and Time Are Causal Transitions". The search: all 2,935 rules rewriting two connected directed relations into three (at most two new nodes) and a random 1,500 rewriting two into four, each run for up to 3,000 updates; the causal graph read by three causal-set dimension tests (ordering fraction, midpoint, longest chain) calibrated on sprinkled flat intervals (3.88, 4.00, 4.02 in four dimensions); 27 rules run to 20,000 updates with size series. Obstacle: a rule that rewrites a few relations at a time gives each update a bounded number of links, and no bounded-valency graph sits on a random causal set without a frame (Bombelli, Henson and Sorkin, 2009), so the match can hold only at large scales. ## SO70 T224 (261012)
- Gary 09:31 (the big picture: the open-source programs are not the Standard Model program we should have; why not just encode GR and QM; is the bounce seen; do the bounce programs keep GR and QM; what is he missing) and 09:35 (is the bounce needed; are we making progress; is it viable; what are we doing; is a faithful program of the formulas possible, and can it fix a black hole's center, or is this wasting time). Answered in the reply, no files changed: this is not that program; a faithful translation inherits the center's infinity, and fixing it takes a rule the formulas lack, which no data can check yet; outside black holes the faithful programs exist (numerical relativity cuts the center out; lattice codes); one program for the whole Standard Model and gravity with real particles is unrealistic (the chiral weak force has not yet run; one proton takes a supercomputer); the runs were small tests of the three known break points (center, left-handed particles, clock), with small real results, and won't unify GR and QM; for matheism the payoff is in (discrete steps remove infinities, the program must choose what the equations leave open, data and shortness narrow it); recommended stopping the gravity work except "frame"; offered "program paragraph" (that result for A41, A38 and Book 4). The bounce: no evidence (inside the horizon; GW250114 matched GR; the June 2026 echo search found none), not the only candidate (regular cores, fuzzballs, gravity weakening at small sizes), the one that comes straight from a smallest step; GR and QM disagree about what space is, and Page and Geilker (1981) with many worlds make gravity quantum. The Mac's family run had not started at 09:33.

## SO70 T225 (261012)
- Gary 09:37 (why the translation from formulas to a program is not straightforward, like unit testing the math; did no one try to adjust the program for the problem cases). Answered in the reply, no files changed: straightforward for each theory in its own domain; four reasons from the math itself: the Standard Model's formulas are defined only as a limit whose existence is unproven (Yang-Mills existence and mass gap, a Millennium Prize problem), the simplest Higgs-type field loses its interaction in that limit (Aizenman and Duminil-Copin, Annals of Mathematics 2021), quantum states grow exponentially (lattice codes compute averages; Feynman's quantum computers), and some formulas change on a grid (Nielsen-Ninomiya 1981; binary black holes first merged in a program in 2005); GR and QM together are not formulas yet. Tried for decades: loop quantum cosmology's bounce (2006), causal dynamical triangulations (Euclidean versions crumpled or branched; four dimensions appeared once every piece had a direction of time, Ambjorn, Jurkiewicz and Loll 2004), causal sets, Wolfram's hypergraphs; none finished. For matheism: the argument (discrete, causal, shortest) is the realistic goal; "program paragraph" now carries the three citations.

## SO70 T226 (261012)
- Gary 09:41 (do programs that translate the Standard Model have the infinities, and would an adjusted one still represent the formulas; can I make one symbol by symbol; is the core problem discrete time breaking the formulas and too many particles to test). Answered in the reply, no files changed: lattice codes are the formulas as a program in discrete space and time (openQCD, Grid; with electromagnetism they matched the neutron-proton mass difference, Borsanyi et al. 2015); the grid removes the infinities by itself; runs at several spacings measure and remove the grid's error; the program samples histories and averages; a box a few protons wide suffices, so too many particles is not the core problem; the gaps are the weak force's left-handed particles (no whole-Standard-Model program yet) and a black hole's center (no formula, nothing comes out to test), which is the core problem; discrete time can break only the rule that no clock is special (Collins et al. 2004), the "frame" question. Offered "lattice demo" (the strong force's gluons on a four-dimensional grid on the Mac, known results only). Gary 09:41 also: he had not yet read the earlier replies.

## SO70 T227 (261012)
- Gary 09:42 (why put the formulas on a grid; is the grid spacetime, since spacetime is not defined another way). Answered in the reply, no files changed: a computer holds finitely many numbers and smooth spacetime has infinitely many points, so a program stands in for it with finitely many (a grid, random points, four-dimensional triangles, or a finite sum of wave shapes); shrinking means more points closer together; the Standard Model takes spacetime as a fixed stage, GR bends it, quantum gravity sums over grids; for matheism the question is whether the grid is a shortcut or real; black-hole entropy (Bekenstein 1973) bounds a region's bits by about a quarter of its area in Planck units, a strong hint that nature needs finitely many numbers per region; if the grid is real there is nothing to shrink. Gary 09:43 (the gluon problem in discrete time, easier? not something he can do): on a grid the gluon theory is well defined, simulations show the mass gap, and on coarse grids it is proven (Osterwalder and Seiler 1978); the Millennium problem asks for the smooth limit; with a real smallest step physics does not need it, a citable point for matheism; agreed he should not take on the proof.

## SO70 T228 (261012)
- Gary 09:46 (simulating QM needs exponentially more power, so quantum computers; an analytic solution might avoid it; for any discrete spacetime we would not know it gives GR without generating it, so a massive or quantum computer?). Answered in the reply, no files changed: partly right; real-time quantum dynamics grows exponentially and quantum computers would remove that (first particle-physics simulations on them, Martinez et al. 2016; still too small and noisy); but whether a rule gives GR can often be shown by math (lattice gases give fluid flow, Frisch, Hasslacher and Pomeau 1986; causal-set curvature averages to Einstein's, Benincasa and Dowker 2010, once space is smooth) or by classical simulation of the geometry in imaginary time (Ambjorn, Jurkiewicz and Loll 2004); the bottleneck is the rule (no special clock, the Standard Model's particles), and hardware comes second; for matheism, a rule's large-scale behavior follows from the rule by math.

## SO70 T238 (261012)
- Gary 11:26 (did Plato know his dualism was a noble lie; what Parmenides meant by nothing changing; why Zeno did not see discrete steps; an illusion of motion still needs steps). Answered: the noble lie is the political myth of the metals (Republic 414b-415d), and nothing shows the Forms were meant as one; Plato split the world to save knowledge from Heraclitean flux (Aristotle, Metaphysics I.6, 987a32-b10) and named the cost himself, the greatest difficulty (Parmenides 133b-134e), keeping the Forms anyway (135b-c); Parmenides came first and Zeno defended him (Parmenides 128c-d); his One is featureless, closer to Plato's One than to a block of steps, though Popper called Einstein "Parmenides" over the block (Unended Quest, 1976); Gary's Book 1 already reconciles them ("Both are right without the split. Becoming is the ordered life of the eternal one"); Zeno did attack steps (the arrow and the stadium, Physics VI.9) and assumed motion passes through every place in between; Epicurus answered with smallest parts (Letter to Herodotus 56), al-Nazzam with the leap, the kalam with discrete moments that God renews; the producer was missing, and matheism puts it in the rule; an illusion of motion is motion in the mind, since seeing takes steps. Applied: A39 answers the arrow and the stadium (with Aristotle's "moved without moving", Physics VI.1, 232a); A3 gives Plato's reason and the greatest difficulty, with Heraclitus's logos (Kahn 1979 added).
